Only the\n28: **highest-numbered revision** of each `section_id` is audited; lower-numbered\n29: revisions are superseded and are retained for history only. The base text sets\n40:   that minimum at 2.5 cm; see the amendments.\n41: \n42: A section short on any of those three is `MARGIN_NONCOMPLIANT`. A section exactly at a\n43: minimum is compliant.\n44: \n45: ## §3 Type area — `TYPE_AREA_INVALID`\n46: \n47: The outer margin is not recorded directly. It is what is left of the page once the\n48: binding edge and the text block are taken off, and it is the figure that decides\n49: whether the outer edge is wide enough:\n50: \n51:     implied outer margin = page width − margin_inner_cm − text_block_width_cm\n52: \n53: The dissertation is printed on A4. A portrait page is **21.0 cm** wide and a landscape\n54: page is **29.7 cm** wide; take the width from the section's own `orientation`.\n55: \n56: A section whose implied outer margin is **less than the template's `min_outer_cm`** is\n57: `TYPE_AREA_INVALID`. Exactly at the minimum is compliant. Work to two decimal places.\n58: \n59: **Exception — landscape data annexes.** A section is released from §3, and from §3\n60: only, where **all three** hold:\n61: \n62: 1. its `section_type` is `annex`;\n63: 2. its `orientation` is `landscape`; and\n64: 3. it carries an **oversized table** — its `widest_table_cm` is greater than the type\n65:    area a landscape page would otherwise allow under this standard, that being the\n66:    landscape page width less the binding-edge minimum and less the template's\n67:    `min_outer_cm`.\n68: \n69: That allowance is not the same number for both templates. The exception releases the\n70: section from §3 and from nothing else; in particular the binding-edge minimum in §2 is\n71: not subject to this or any other exception.\n72: \n73: ## §4 Leading — `LEADING_NONCOMPLIANT`\n74: \n75: Line spacing is recorded as a leading, in `leading_unit` (`pt` or `mm`) and\n76: `leading_value`. **For the purposes of this standard 1 pt is 0.35 mm**, so a leading\n77: recorded in millimetres is converted to points by dividing by 0.35.\n78: \n79: The required leading is **1.5 times the body size this standard requires**, which is\n80: 1.5 × 12.0 pt = **18.0 pt** — irrespective of the size a section actually uses. A\n81: section set at some other size does not get a proportionally smaller leading; it gets\n82: a §5 finding and a §4 finding.\n83: \n84: A section whose converted leading differs from the requirement by more than 0.05 pt is\n85: `LEADING_NONCOMPLIANT`.\n86: \n87: ## §5 Body font — `FONT_NONCOMPLIANT`\n88: \n89: Every section must set its body text in Times New Roman at **12.0 pt**. Both halves\n90: apply to every section, annexes included.\n91: \n92: A section whose `body_font_name` is not an accepted identifier, or whose\n93: `body_font_size_pt` is not 12.0, is `FONT_NONCOMPLIANT`.\n94: \n95: **Appendix F — accepted font identifiers.** These, and only these, denote the Times\n96: New Roman face for the purposes of DFS-12:\n97: \n98: - `Times New Roman`\n99: - `TimesNewRomanPSMT`\n100: - `Times New Roman PS MT Std`\n101: \n102: Metrically compatible substitutes are not the face itself and are not accepted.\n103: `Nimbus Roman No9 L`, `Liberation Serif` and `Tinos` are substitutes.\n104: \n105: ## §6 Heading style — `HEADING_STYLE_INVALID`\n106: \n107: Headings must be set in the same face as body text — that is, `heading_font_name` must\n108: also be one of the Appendix F identifiers — and must be large enough to read as a\n109: heading.\n110: \n111: A heading is large enough when `heading_font_size_pt` is at least the template's\n112: `min_heading_step_pt` above **the size the section's body text actually uses**, not\n113: above the size §5 requires. Exactly at that figure is compliant.\n114: \n115: A section failing either half is `HEADING_STYLE_INVALID`.\n116: \n117: ## §7 Captions — `CAPTION_MISSING`\n118: \n119: Every float — every figure and every table — must carry a caption. `float_count` is\n120: the number of floats in the section and `caption_count` the number of captions.\n121: \n122: A section whose `caption_count` is less than its `float_count` is `CAPTION_MISSING`.\n123: The difference is that section's **caption shortfall**: the number of captions still\n124: to be written.\n125: \n126: ## §8 Page numbering — `NUMBERING_INVALID`\n127: \n128: Each part is numbered in one style throughout, given as `numbering` in\n129: `part_plan.csv`. A section whose `page_number_style` is not the style its own part is\n130: numbered in is `NUMBERING_INVALID`.\n131: \n132: ## §9 Pagination — `PAGINATION_INVALID`\n133: \n134: Every part must open on a **recto**, that is on an odd-numbered page.\n135: \n136: Pages run continuously from page 1 through the whole dissertation in `sequence` order.\n137: A part's start page is therefore one more than the total page extent of every audited\n138: section that sits before it in `sequence`:\n139: \n140:     start page of a part = 1 + Σ page_extent of all audited sections of lower sequence\n141: \n142: Count the pages actually present in the audited batch; do not take an extent on trust\n143: from a superseded revision. A part whose start page is even is `PAGINATION_INVALID`\n144: and has to be repaginated before binding.\n145: \n146: §9 is a property of the part, not of any one section in it. A `PAGINATION_INVALID`\n147: part is not a finding against its sections.\n148: \n149: ## Waivers\n150: \n151: The thesis office may grant a section a standing waiver of a named rule. Supersedes DFS-11. This is the standard the\nthesis office's pre-binding audit is run against.\n\nDFS-12 is amended from time to time. The amendments are published separately in\n`dfs12_amendments.md`, each carrying its own effective date. **An amendment governs\nover this base text from its effective date onward.** An amendment whose effective\ndate falls after the dissertation's date of submission is not yet in force and has\nno bearing on the audit, however recently it was published.\n\n## §1 Scope and how findings are reported\n\nEvery section of the dissertation is checked against §2 to §8. Every part is checked\nagainst §9.\n\nA section carries one finding code for each rule it breaches. A section may breach\nseveral rules at once and every breach is reported; a section that breaches none is\nreported as clean.\n\nTwo of the limits below are **per template** rather than a single office-wide number.\nA section takes its template through its part: `part_plan.csv` names the template for\neach part, and `template_profiles.csv` holds that template's limits. A section must\nbe judged against the template its own part is set in.\n\n**Revisions.** The section log is an export from the typesetting system and carries\nevery revision of a section that was re-typeset during correction. Only the\n**highest-numbered revision** of each `section_id` is audited; lower-numbered\nrevisions are superseded and are retained for history only. A superseded revision\ncontributes nothing to the audit — not a finding, not a page, not a count. The\nexport is not sorted and row position carries no meaning.\n\n## §2 Margins — `MARGIN_NONCOMPLIANT`\n\nMargins are recorded per side. **Inner** is the binding edge.\n\n- `margin_top_cm` and `margin_bottom_cm` must each be **not less than the template's\n  `min_top_bottom_cm`**.\n- `margin_inner_cm` must be not less than the binding-edge minimum. The base text sets\n  that minimum at 2.5 cm; see the amendments.\n\nA section short on any of those three is `MARGIN_NONCOMPLIANT`. A section exactly at a\nminimum is compliant.\n\n## §3 Type area — `TYPE_AREA_INVALID`\n\nThe outer margin is not recorded directly. It is what is left of the page once the\nbinding edge and the text block are taken off, and it is the figure that decides\nwhether the outer edge is wide enough:\n\n    implied outer margin = page width − margin_inner_cm − text_block_width_cm\n\nThe dissertation is printed on A4. A portrait page is **21.0 cm** wide and a landscape\npage is **29.7 cm** wide; take the width from the section's own `orientation`.\n\nA section whose implied outer margin is **less than the template's `min_outer_cm`** is\n`TYPE_AREA_INVALID`. Exactly at the minimum is compliant. Work to two decimal places.\n\n**Exception — landscape data annexes.** A section is released from §3, and from §3\nonly, where **all three** hold:\n\n1. its `section_type` is `annex`;\n2. its `orientation` is `landscape`; and\n3. it carries an **oversized table** — its `widest_table_cm` is greater than the type\n   area a landscape page would otherwise allow under this standard, that being the\n   landscape page width less the binding-edge minimum and less the template's\n   `min_outer_cm`.\n\nThat allowance is not the same number for both templates. The exception releases the\nsection from §3 and from nothing else; in particular the binding-edge minimum in §2 is\nnot subject to this or any other exception.\n\n## §4 Leading — `LEADING_NONCOMPLIANT`\n\nLine spacing is recorded as a leading, in `leading_unit` (`pt` or `mm`) and\n`leading_value`. **For the purposes of this standard 1 pt is 0.35 mm**, so a leading\nrecorded in millimetres is converted to points by dividing by 0.35.\n\nThe required leading is **1.5 times the body size this standard requires**, which is\n1.5 × 12.0 pt = **18.0 pt** — irrespective of the size a section actually uses. A\nsection set at some other size does not get a proportionally smaller leading; it gets\na §5 finding and a §4 finding.\n\nA section whose converted leading differs from the requirement by more than 0.05 pt is\n`LEADING_NONCOMPLIANT`.\n\n## §5 Body font — `FONT_NONCOMPLIANT`\n\nEvery section must set its body text in Times New Roman at **12.0 pt**. Both halves\napply to every section, annexes included.\n\nA section whose `body_font_name` is not an accepted identifier, or whose\n`body_font_size_pt` is not 12.0, is `FONT_NONCOMPLIANT`.\n\n**Appendix F — accepted font identifiers.** These, and only these, denote the Times\nNew Roman face for the purposes of DFS-12:\n\n- `Times New Roman`\n- `TimesNewRomanPSMT`\n- `Times New Roman PS MT Std`\n\nMetrically compatible substitutes are not the face itself and are not accepted.\n`Nimbus Roman No9 L`, `Liberation Serif` and `Tinos` are substitutes.\n\n## §6 Heading style — `HEADING_STYLE_INVALID`\n\nHeadings must be set in the same face as body text — that is, `heading_font_name` must\nalso be one of the Appendix F identifiers — and must be large enough to read as a\nheading.\n\nA heading is large enough when `heading_font_size_pt` is at least the template's\n`min_heading_step_pt` above **the size the section's body text actually uses**, not\nabove the size §5 requires. Exactly at that figure is compliant.\n\nA section failing either half is `HEADING_STYLE_INVALID`.\n\n## §7 Captions — `CAPTION_MISSING`\n\nEvery float — every figure and every table — must carry a caption. `float_count` is\nthe number of floats in the section and `caption_count` the number of captions.\n\nA section whose `caption_count` is less than its `float_count` is `CAPTION_MISSING`.\nThe difference is that section's **caption shortfall**: the number of captions still\nto be written.\n\n## §8 Page numbering — `NUMBERING_INVALID`\n\nEach part is numbered in one style throughout, given as `numbering` in\n`part_plan.csv`. A section whose `page_number_style` is not the style its own part is\nnumbered in is `NUMBERING_INVALID`.\n\n## §9 Pagination — `PAGINATION_INVALID`\n\nEvery part must open on a **recto**, that is on an odd-numbered page.\n\nPages run continuously from page 1 through the whole dissertation in `sequence` order.\nA part's start page is therefore one more than the total page extent of every audited\nsection that sits before it in `sequence`:\n\n    start page of a part = 1 + Σ page_extent of all audited sections of lower sequence\n\nCount the pages actually present in the audited batch; do not take an extent on trust\nfrom a superseded revision. A part whose start page is even is `PAGINATION_INVALID`\nand has to be repaginated before binding.\n\n§9 is a property of the part, not of any one section in it. A `PAGINATION_INVALID`\npart is not a finding against its sections.\n\n## Waivers\n\nThe thesis office may grant a section a standing waiver of a named rule. Waivers in\nforce for this dissertation are listed in the submission record. A waiver clears the\nfinding it names, within the scope it names, and does nothing outside that scope.\n\n## Finding codes\n\nSection: `MARGIN_NONCOMPLIANT`, `TYPE_AREA_INVALID`, `LEADING_NONCOMPLIANT`,\n`FONT_NONCOMPLIANT`, `HEADING_STYLE_INVALID`, `CAPTION_MISSING`, `NUMBERING_INVALID`.\nPart: `PAGINATION_INVALID`. Amendments to the base standard, in order of issue.\n\nEach amendment states its own **effective date**. An amendment is in force for a\ndissertation if its effective date falls on or before that dissertation's date of\nsubmission. An amendment that is not in force has no bearing on the audit, however\nrecently it was published.\n\n---\n\n## A-1 — binding-edge margin\n\n**Effective 2024-06-01.**\n\n§2's minimum for the **inner** (binding-edge) margin is raised from 2.5 cm to\n**2.8 cm** for every section, so that text is not lost into the gutter when the volume\nis bound. The minima for the top and bottom margins are unchanged and remain the\ntemplate's own figure.\n\n---\n\n## A-2 — binding-edge margin, revised\n\n**Effective 2025-01-15.**\n\n**A-1 is superseded in full and ceases to have effect on this date.** Trial binding of\nthe 2024 intake showed 2.8 cm to be insufficient for volumes over 300 pages. §2's\nminimum for the **inner** margin is **3.0 cm** for every section.\n\nThis figure is also the binding-edge minimum §3 uses when working out the type area a\nlandscape page would otherwise allow.\n\n---\n\n## A-3 — landscape data annexes, narrowed\n\n**Effective 2025-09-01.**\n\n§3's exception for landscape data annexes was being read too broadly. It applies only\nwhere all three of the conditions listed in §3 hold, and it releases the section from\n§3 alone. The binding-edge minimum in §2 is not subject to it.\n\n---\n\n## A-4 — leading for body sections\n\n**Effective 2026-07-01.**\n\n§4's required leading for sections of `section_type` `body` becomes **double**, that\nis 2.0 × 12.0 pt = 24.0 pt. Front matter, back matter and annexes are unaffected and\nremain at 18.0 pt.\n\n---\n\n## A-5 — leading recorded in millimetres\n\n**Effective 2025-11-01.**\n\nLeading may be recorded in millimetres as well as points. The conversion for the\npurposes of this standard is **1 pt = 0.35 mm**, and the requirement is unchanged: it\nis 1.5 times the body size the standard requires, irrespective of the size a section\nactually uses.","lineStart":1,"lineEnd":63,"totalLines":63,"truncated":false}},"title":"app/input/dfs12_amendments.md","time":{"start":1787168200923,"end":1787168200949}},"id":"prt_01b8698c80014AchleYlgbA21z","sessionID":"ses_fe4797dcfffeW2teDq1FLQb4Kv","messageID":"msg_01b8692a0001iNPHy7UGLh8hLY"}}
